I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to determine if a string S can be written in the form ABABA where A and B are non-empty strings
2. If possible, output "Love AB!" with the pair (A,B) that has the smallest |AB| (length of A+B)
3. If not possible, output "mitomerarenaiWA"

## Key Insights

1. For S = ABABA, the string has a specific pattern: A-B-A-B-A
2. The length of S must be 5k where k ≥ 1 (since we have 5 repeating blocks)
3. If |S| = n, then A and B must satisfy: n = 5k, where k = len(A) + len(B)
4. We need to find valid A and B such that when we repeat ABABA, we get the original string

## Analysis of the Pattern

For S = ABABA:
- Positions 0 to k-1: A
- Positions k to 2k-1: B  
- Positions 2k to 3k-1: A
- Positions 3k to 4k-1: B
- Positions 4k to 5k-1: A

So for each possible length k = len(A) + len(B), we need:
- A[0:k] = S[0:k]
- B[0:k] = S[k:2k] 
- A[0:k] = S[2k:3k]
- B[0:k] = S[3k:4k]
- A[0:k] = S[4k:5k]

## Algorithm Steps

1. Check if the length of S is divisible by 5
2. For each possible k from 1 to n/5:
   - Calculate the length of A: a = k - b, where b is length of B
   - Actually, we need to try different values of k (length of AB)
   - For each k from 1 to n//5:
     - Extract the 5 segments of length k each
     - Check if they all match the pattern (A-B-A-B-A)
     - If yes, we found a valid solution
3. Return the first valid (A,B) pair with smallest k

Wait, let me reconsider. Since we want the pair with smallest |AB|, we should try from smallest k to largest.

Actually, let me think more clearly:
- If |S| = n, then n = 5k where k = |AB|
- We need to find k such that 5k = n and the string follows ABABA pattern
- For each valid k, we check if S[0:k] == S[2k:3k] == S[4k:5k] and S[k:2k] == S[3k:4k]

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
s = input().strip()
n = len(s)

# Check if length is divisible by 5
if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    k = n // 5  # k = length of AB
    
    # We need to check if s can be written as ABABA
    # where A = s[0:k-?], B = s[?] and k = len(A) + len(B)
    # Actually, let me rethink: let len(A) = a, len(B) = b, so k = a + b
    
    # We need to find valid a and b where a + b = k
    # and the string follows ABABA pattern
    
    # The pattern is:
    # positions 0 to a-1: A
    # positions a to a+b-1: B
    # positions a+b to 2a+b-1: A
    # positions 2a+b to 3a+2b-1: B
    # positions 3a+2b to 4a+3b-1: A
    
    # Since we want smallest |AB| = a + b, we can try all possible values of a from 1 to k-1
    # and b = k - a
    
    found = False
    a = 1
    b = k - 1
    
    # Try all possible values of a from 1 to k-1
    for a in range(1, k):
        b = k - a
        if b <= 0:
            break
            
        # Check if the string follows ABABA pattern
        # A should be s[0:a] and also s[2a:2a+b] and s[4a:4a+b]
        # B should be s[a:a+b] and also s[2a+b:3a+2b] and s[3a+2b:4a+3b]
        
        # Extract A and B
        A = s[0:a]
        B = s[a:a+b]
        
        # Check positions
        # First A: indices 0 to a-1
        # First B: indices a to a+b-1
        # Second A: indices a+b to a+b+a-1 = 2a+b to 2a+2b-1
        # Second B: indices 2a+2b to 3a+3b-1
        # Third A: indices 3a+3b to 4a+4b-1
        
        # Position 0 to a-1: A
        # Position a to a+b-1: B
        # Position a+b to 2a+b-1: A
        # Position 2a+2b to 3a+3b-1: B
        # Position 3a+3b to 4a+4b-1: A
        
        # Actually, positions:
        # A: 0 to a-1, a+b to 2a+b-1, 2a+2b to 3a+3b-1
        # B: a to a+b-1, 2a+2b to 3a+3b-1
        
        # Let me correct: positions are:
        # A: 0 to a-1 (first A)
        # B: a to a+b-1 (first B)
        # A: a+b to a+b+a-1 = 2a+b to 2a+2b-1 (second A)  
        # B: 2a+2b to 3a+3b-1 (second B)
        # A: 3a+3b to 4a+4b-1 (third A)
        
        # Check each segment
        pos_A1 = 0
        pos_A2 = a + b
        pos_A3 = 2*a + 2*b
        
        pos_B1 = a
        pos_B2 = 2*a + 2*b
        
        # Check A segments
        if s[pos_A1:pos_A1 + a] != A or s[pos_A2:pos_A2 + a] != A or s[pos_A3:pos_A3 + a] != A:
            continue
            
        # Check B segments
        if s[pos_B1:pos_B1 + b] != B or s[pos_B2:pos_B2 + b] != B:
            continue
            
        # Found valid pair
        print(f"Love {A}!")
        found = True
        break
    
    if not found:
        print("mitomerarenaiWA")
```

Wait, I think I made an error in the position calculation. Let me rework this.

For ABABA:
- A appears at positions 0 to a-1, a+b to a+b+a-1, 2a+2b to 2a+2b+a-1
- B appears at positions a to a+b-1, 2a+2b to 2a+2b+b-1

The total length should be 5k = 5(a+b).

Let me implement this correctly:

```python
s = input().strip()
n = len(s)

# Check if length is divisible by 5
if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    k = n // 5  # k = length of AB
    
    # Try all possible values of a (length of A) from 1 to k-1
    # b = k - a (length of B)
    found = False
    
    for a in range(1, k):
        b = k - a
        
        # Check if the string can be split as ABABA
        # A: s[0:a], s[a+b:a+b+a], s[2*(a+b):2*(a+b)+a]
        # B: s[a:a+b], s[a+2*(a+b):a+2*(a+b)+b]
        
        # Position of first A: 0 to a-1
        # Position of first B: a to a+b-1  
        # Position of second A: a+b to a+b+a-1
        # Position of second B: a+2*(a+b) to a+2*(a+b)+b-1
        # Position of third A: a+3*(a+b) to a+3*(a+b)+a-1
        
        # Actually, positions:
        # First A: 0 to a-1
        # First B: a to a+b-1
        # Second A: a+b to a+b+a-1 = 2a+b to 2a+2b-1
        # Second B: 2a+2b to 2a+2b+b-1 = 2a+3b to 2a+3b+b-1
        # Third A: 3a+3b to 3a+3b+a-1 = 4a+4b to 4a+4b+a-1
        
        # Calculate positions:
        pos_A1 = 0
        pos_A2 = a + b
        pos_A3 = 2*(a + b)
        
        pos_B1 = a
        pos_B2 = 2*(a + b)
        
        # Check the segments
        A = s[0:a]
        B = s[a:a+b]
        
        # Check if A matches at positions 0, a+b, 2(a+b)
        if (s[pos_A1:pos_A1+a] != A or 
            s[pos_A2:pos_A2+a] != A or 
            s[pos_A3:pos_A3+a] != A):
            continue
            
        # Check if B matches at positions a, 2(a+b)
        if (s[pos_B1:pos_B1+b] != B or 
            s[pos_B2:pos_B2+b] != B):
            continue
            
        # Found valid pair with smallest AB length
        print(f"Love {A}!")
        found = True
        break
    
    if not found:
        print("mitomerarenaiWA")
```

Wait, I think I'm still making an error in position calculation. Let me restart and be very careful.

For ABABA with A of length a and B of length b:
- Length: 5(a+b) = n
- Positions:
  - 0 to a-1: A
  - a to a+b-1: B  
  - a+b to a+b+a-1: A
  - a+b+a to a+b+a+b-1: B
  - a+b+a+b to a+b+a+b+a-1: A

So:
- First A: 0 to a-1
- First B: a to a+b-1
- Second A: a+b to a+b+a-1
- Second B: a+b+a to a+b+a+b-1
- Third A: a+b+a+b to a+b+a+b+a-1

Therefore:
- First A: s[0:a]
- First B: s[a:a+b]
- Second A: s[a+b:a+b+a]
- Second B: s[a+b+a:a+b+a+b]
- Third A: s[a+b+a+b:a+b+a+b+a]

```python
s = input().strip()

    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:

    # b = k - a (length of B)

        A1 = s[0:a]
        # Second A  
        A2 = s[a+b:a+b+a]
        # Third A
        A3 = s[a+b+a+b:a+b+a+b+a]
        
        # First B
        B1 = s[a:a+b]
        # Second B
        B2 = s[a+b+a:a+b+a+b]

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

F: Miko Mi String-

story

Mikko Mikkomi ~! Everyone's idol, Miko Miko Tazawa! Today ~, with Mikoto ~, practice the string algorithm, let's do it ☆

Miko's special ~~ character making ~~ The slogan "MikoMikomi" becomes "MikoMikoMi" in Roman letters! In other words, if A = “Mi” and B = “Ko”, you can write in the form of ABABA! In this way, a character string that can be decomposed into the form of ABABA by properly determining A and B is called "Mikomi character string"! Miko is a popular person for everyone to become the name of a character string!

In order for everyone to use the Mikomi character string, I decided to make a program to judge whether the given character string is the Mikomi character string, but I can't write a program longer than Miko and FizzBuzz. !! So ~, for Miko ~, I want you to write a program that judges Mikomi character strings ☆

...... You said it's cold now! ??

problem

A character string S consisting of uppercase and lowercase letters is given. Here, if there are two non-empty strings A and B that can be written as S = ABABA, then S is said to be a "Mikomi string". At this time, uppercase and lowercase letters of the alphabet shall be distinguished as different characters. Create a program that determines whether a given string is a string.

Input format

The input consists of only one line including the character string S. It can be assumed that S satisfies the following conditions.

* 1 ≤ | S | ≤ 10 ^ 6. However, | S | represents the length of the character string S.
* S consists only of uppercase or lowercase alphabets.



Since the input may be very large, it is recommended to use a high-speed function to receive the input.

Output format

If S is a character string, output "Love AB!" For A and B that satisfy S = ABABA. However, if multiple pairs of A and B satisfy the condition, output the one with the smallest | AB |. If S is not a Mikomi string, output "mitomerarenaiWA".

Input example 1


NicoNicoNi

Output example 1


Love Nico!

Input example 2


Kashikoi Kawaii Elichika

Output example 2


mitomerarenaiWA

LiveLiveL

Output example 3


Love Live!

AizunyanPeroPero

Output example 4


mitomerarenaiWA

Input example 5


AAAAAAAAAAAAAA

Output example 5


Love AAAAA!

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to determine if a string S can be written in the form ABABA where A and B are non-empty strings
2. If such A and B exist, we output "Love AB!" with the pair that has the smallest |AB| (length of A+B)
3. If no such pair exists, we output "mitomerarenaiWA"

## Key Insights

For a string to be of the form ABABA:
- The total length must be 5k where k is some positive integer (since ABABA has pattern repeated 3 times)
- The pattern is: A + B + A + B + A
- This means positions 0,2,4 have A characters
- Positions 1,3 have B characters
- So for each valid division point, we have: S[0:len(A)] = A, S[len(A):len(A)+len(B)] = B, and so on

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. First, check if the length of S is divisible by 5. If not, it's impossible.
2. Let n = len(S). Then the pattern length (AB) must be n/5.
3. For each possible length of A from 1 to n/5, we can determine the length of B as (n/5) - A.
4. Check if the string follows the pattern ABABA:
   - First A: positions 0 to len(A)-1
   - First B: positions len(A) to len(A)+len(B)-1
   - Second A: positions len(A)+len(B) to len(A)+len(B)+len(A)-1
   - Second B: positions len(A)+len(B)+len(A) to len(A)+len(B)+len(A)+len(B)-1
   - Third A: positions len(A)+len(B)+len(A)+len(B) to end
5. We want the pair with smallest |AB|, so we start from the smallest possible A.

## Implementation

```python
S = input().strip()
n = len(S)

# Check if length is divisible by 5
if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    # The length of AB should be n//5
    ab_len = n // 5
    
    # Try all possible lengths of A from 1 to ab_len-1
    # We want the smallest |AB|, so we start from smallest A
    found = False
    
    for a_len in range(1, ab_len):
        b_len = ab_len - a_len
        
        # Check if this split works
        if b_len <= 0:
            continue
            
        # Pattern: ABABA
        # A: 0 to a_len-1
        # B: a_len to a_len+b_len-1
        # A: a_len+b_len to a_len+b_len+a_len-1
        # B: a_len+b_len+a_len to a_len+b_len+a_len+b_len-1  
        # A: a_len+b_len+a_len+b_len+a_len to end
        
        # Check first A
        if S[:a_len] != S[a_len + b_len : a_len + b_len + a_len]:
            continue
            
        # Check first B
        if S[a_len:a_len + b_len] != S[a_len + b_len + a_len : a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len]:
            continue
            
        # Check second A
        if S[a_len + b_len : a_len + b_len + a_len] != S[a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len : a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len + a_len]:
            continue
            
        # Check third A
        if S[a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len : a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len + a_len] != S[a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len + a_len :]:
            continue
            
        # If we reach here, we found a valid AB
        A = S[:a_len]
        B = S[a_len:a_len + b_len]
        print(f"Love {A}{B}!")
        found = True
        break
    
    if not found:
        print("mitomerarenaiWA")
```

Wait, I think I made an error in indexing. Let me reconsider the pattern:

For ABABA:
- A: indices 0 to a_len-1
- B: indices a_len to a_len+b_len-1  
- A: indices a_len+b_len to a_len+b_len+a_len-1
- B: indices a_len+b_len+a_len to a_len+b_len+a_len+b_len-1
- A: indices a_len+b_len+a_len+b_len+a_len to end

Let me fix this:

```python
S = input().strip()
n = len(S)

# Check if length is divisible by 5
if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    # The length of AB should be n//5
    ab_len = n // 5
    
    # Try all possible lengths of A from 1 to ab_len-1
    # We want the smallest |AB|, so we start from smallest A
    found = False
    
    for a_len in range(1, ab_len):
        b_len = ab_len - a_len
        
        # Check if this split works
        if b_len <= 0:
            continue
            
        # Check ABABA pattern
        # Position 0: A
        # Position 1: B
        # Position 2: A
        # Position 3: B
        # Position 4: A
        
        # First A: 0 to a_len-1
        first_A = S[:a_len]
        
        # First B: a_len to a_len+b_len-1
        first_B = S[a_len:a_len + b_len]
        
        # Second A: a_len+b_len to a_len+b_len+a_len-1
        second_A = S[a_len + b_len : a_len + b_len + a_len]
        
        # Second B: a_len+b_len+a_len to a_len+b_len+a_len+b_len-1
        second_B = S[a_len + b_len + a_len : a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len]
        
        # Third A: a_len+b_len+a_len+b_len+a_len to end
        third_A = S[a_len + b_len + a_len + b_len + a_len:]
        
        # Check if all A parts are equal and all B parts are equal
        if (first_A == second_A == third_A and 
            first_B == second_B):
            A = S[:a_len]
            B = S[a_len:a_len + b_len]
            print(f"Love {A}{B}!")
            found = True
            break
    
    if not found:
        print("mitomerarenaiWA")

The integers 1, 2, 3, and 4 representing the ticket types represent S seats, A seats, B seats, and C seats, respectively. Numbers from 1 to 4 always appear once as values ​​for t1, t2, t3, and t4, but they are not always given in the order of 1, 2, 3, 4.

output

3 10

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing:
   - Ticket type (1-4) 
   - Number of tickets sold

3. The ticket types don't appear in order, so we need to map each type to its price and calculate the total sales amount for each row.

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Define the price mapping
Create a dictionary to map ticket types to their prices:
- Type 1 (S) → 6000 yen
- Type 2 (A) → 4000 yen
- Type 3 (B) → 3000 yen
- Type 4 (C) → 2000 yen

### Step 2: Process each line
For each line of input:
- Extract the ticket type and number of tickets
- Look up the price for that ticket type
- Calculate the sales amount (price × number of tickets)
- Output the result

### Step 3: Implementation

```python
# Read input and process each line
price_map = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Process each of the 4 lines
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    number_of_tickets = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount for this row
    sales_amount = price_map[ticket_type] * number_of_tickets
    
    # Output the result
    print(sales_amount)
```

## Verification with Example

Input:
- "3 10" → Type 3 (B seat) × 10 = 3000 × 10 = 30000 ✓
- "1 4" → Type 1 (S seat) × 4 = 6000 × 4 = 24000 ✓
- "4 1" → Type 4 (C seat) × 1 = 2000 × 1 = 2000 ✓
- "2 5" → Type 2 (A seat) × 5 = 4000 × 5 = 20000 ✓

The output matches the expected result.

    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Process each of the 4 lines
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    number_of_tickets = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount for this row
    sales_amount = price_map[ticket_type] * number_of_tickets
    
    # Output the result
    print(sales_amount)

   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing a ticket type (1-4) and the number of tickets sold for that type.

3. We need to calculate the sales amount for each row (ticket type × quantity).

4. The ticket types aren't necessarily given in order (1, 2, 3, 4), so we need to map each type to its price.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Identify the price mapping**: Create a dictionary to map ticket types to their prices
   - Type 1 → 6000 yen (S seat)
   - Type 2 → 4000 yen (A seat)
   - Type 3 → 3000 yen (B seat)
   - Type 4 → 2000 yen (C seat)

2. **Process each input line**: For each line, extract the ticket type and quantity, then calculate sales amount = price × quantity

3. **Output the result**: Print each sales amount on a separate line

## Implementation

```python
# Create price mapping for each ticket type
ticket_prices = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Process each of the 4 input lines
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    quantity = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount for this row
    sales_amount = ticket_prices[ticket_type] * quantity
    
    # Output the result
    print(sales_amount)
```

## Verification with Example

Input:
- 3 10 → Type 3 (B seat) × 10 = 3000 × 10 = 30000 ✓
- 1 4 → Type 1 (S seat) × 4 = 6000 × 4 = 24000 ✓
- 4 1 → Type 4 (C seat) × 1 = 2000 × 1 = 2000 ✓
- 2 5 → Type 2 (A seat) × 5 = 4000 × 5 = 20000 ✓

Output matches the expected result exactly.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing a ticket type (1-4) and the number of tickets sold.

3. The ticket types are not necessarily given in order (S, A, B, C), so we need to map each type to its price.

4. For each line, we need to calculate the sales amount = (price per ticket) × (number of tickets).

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Map ticket types to prices**: Create a dictionary to map type numbers to their corresponding prices
   - Type 1 → 6000 yen
   - Type 2 → 4000 yen  
   - Type 3 → 3000 yen
   - Type 4 → 2000 yen

2. **Process each input line**: For each line containing type and count:
   - Look up the price for that ticket type
   - Multiply price by count to get sales amount
   - Output the result

3. **Output format**: Print each sales amount on a separate line in the same order as the input.

## Verification with Example

Input:
- 3 10 → B seat: 3000 × 10 = 30,000 ✓
- 1 4 → S seat: 6000 × 4 = 24,000 ✓
- 4 1 → C seat: 2000 × 1 = 2,000 ✓
- 2 5 → A seat: 4000 × 5 = 20,000 ✓

Output matches expected: 30000, 24000, 2000, 20000

## Code Implementation

```python
# Create price mapping for ticket types
ticket_prices = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Process each line of input
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    ticket_count = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount
    sales_amount = ticket_prices[ticket_type] * ticket_count
    print(sales_amount)
```

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing a ticket type (1-4) and the number of tickets sold for that type.

3. The ticket types are not given in order (1, 2, 3, 4), so we need to map each type to its price and calculate the sales amount for each row.

4. For each row, we need to output the total sales amount (price × quantity).

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Map ticket types to prices**: Create a dictionary to store the price for each ticket type
2. **Process each input line**: For each line, extract the ticket type and quantity
3. **Calculate sales amount**: Multiply the price by the quantity for each row
4. **Output the result**: Print the sales amount for each row

## Implementation

```python
# Map ticket types to their prices
# Type 1 = S seat = 6000 yen
# Type 2 = A seat = 4000 yen  
# Type 3 = B seat = 3000 yen
# Type 4 = C seat = 2000 yen

price_map = {
    1: 6000,
    2: 4000,
    3: 3000,
    4: 2000
}

# Read and process each line
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    quantity = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount for this row
    sales_amount = price_map[ticket_type] * quantity
    
    # Output the result
    print(sales_amount)
```

- "1 4": Type 1 (S seat) × 4 = 6000 × 4 = 24000
- "4 1": Type 4 (C seat) × 1 = 2000 × 1 = 2000
- "2 5": Type 2 (A seat) × 5 = 4000 × 5 = 20000

   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

4. For each line, we need to output the sales amount (price × quantity).

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Define the price mapping
Create a dictionary to map ticket types (1-4) to their prices:
- Type 1 → 6000 yen (S seat)
- Type 2 → 4000 yen (A seat)
- Type 3 → 3000 yen (B seat)
- Type 4 → 2000 yen (C seat)

### Step 2: Read and process each line
For each of the 4 lines:
- Read the ticket type and quantity
- Look up the price for that type
- Calculate sales amount = price × quantity
- Output the result

- 1 4 (S seat, 4 tickets) → 6000 × 4 = 24,000
- 4 1 (C seat, 1 ticket) → 2000 × 1 = 2,000
- 2 5 (A seat, 5 tickets) → 4000 × 5 = 20,000

    quantity = int(line[1])

    print(sales_amount)

1. Create a price dictionary to easily map ticket types to prices
2. Read each line, extract the ticket type and quantity
3. Look up the price and multiply by quantity to get sales amount
4. Print the result for each line

Time complexity: O(1) - constant time for each operation
Space complexity: O(1) - only storing the price map which has fixed size

Numbers from 1 to 4 always appear once as values ​​for t1, t2, t3, and t4, but they are not always given in the order of 1, 2, 3, 4.

output

Example

Input

1 4
4 1
2 5


Output

24000

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We get 4 lines of input, each containing a ticket type (1-4) and the number of tickets sold for that type.

3. We need to calculate the sales amount for each line (type × quantity).

4. The ticket types might not appear in order (1, 2, 3, 4), so we need to map each type to its price.

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Define the price mapping
Create a dictionary or mapping that associates each ticket type with its price:
- Type 1 (S) → 6000 yen
- Type 2 (A) → 4000 yen
- Type 3 (B) → 3000 yen
- Type 4 (C) → 2000 yen

### Step 2: Process each line
For each line with format "t n":
- Extract ticket type t and quantity n
- Look up the price for type t
- Calculate sales amount = price × quantity
- Output the result

### Step 3: Implementation

```python
# Read the 4 lines of input
price_map = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Process each line and calculate sales amount
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    quantity = int(line[1])
    
    price = price_map[ticket_type]
    sales_amount = price * quantity
    
    print(sales_amount)
```

## Verification with Example

Input:
```
3 10
1 4
4 1
2 5
```

Calculations:
- Type 3 (B seat): 3000 × 10 = 30,000 ✓
- Type 1 (S seat): 6000 × 4 = 24,000 ✓
- Type 4 (C seat): 2000 × 1 = 2,000 ✓
- Type 2 (A seat): 4000 × 5 = 20,000 ✓

Output matches expected result.
